### DOCX Table Index 2 (`table_abbr`): Summary of Domain Abbreviations and Technical Acronyms in RAG2ATT&CK.
| Acronym | Full Meaning & Technical Context in RAG2ATT&CK |
|---|---|
| AI / ML / NLP | Artificial Intelligence / Machine Learning / Natural Language Processing |
| APT | Advanced Persistent Threat |
| ATT&CK | Adversarial Tactics, Techniques, and Common Knowledge (MITRE Enterprise Matrix v19.2) |
| BM25 | Best Matching 25 (Lexical bag-of-words retrieval ranking function) |
| CI | Confidence Interval (95% Cluster Bootstrap over 440 scenario clusters) / Continuous Integration |
| CP / HOD402 / OJT | Capstone Project / Course HOD402 / On-the-Job Training (FPT University) |
| CTI | Cyber Threat Intelligence |
| DEV / TEST | Development Split (30 pairs / 60 views) / Canonical Test Split (640 pairs / 1,280 views; 718 scorable) |
| eBPF | Extended Berkeley Packet Filter (Linux kernel telemetry comparator) |
| EDR / SIEM | Endpoint Detection and Response / Security Information and Event Management |
| FAISS | Facebook AI Similarity Search (IndexFlatIP exact inner-product vector index) |
| FN / FP / TP | False Negative / False Positive / True Positive |
| FWER | Family-Wise Error Rate |
| GT | Ground Truth (single-GT = 1 technique; multi-GT = multiple concurrent techniques) |
| Hit@k / Recall@k | Sample-Level Any-Hit Rate (Hit@k, ANY_GT_RETRIEVED) vs. Fractional Multi-Label Macro Recall@k |
| HR / MAP / MRR / NDCG | Hit Rate / Mean Average Precision / Mean Reciprocal Rank / Normalized Discounted Cumulative Gain |
| IA / IDS | Information Assurance / Intrusion Detection System |
| JSON / XML | JavaScript Object Notation / Extensible Markup Language |
| KB | Knowledge Base (474 active Windows ATT&CK techniques & sub-techniques) |
| LADE | LLM-Assisted Advanced Persistent Threat Detection and Explanation (Gwak et al., 2026/2027) |
| LLM | Large Language Model (Fixed OpenAI gpt-5.6-luna alias with reasoning_effort=xhigh) |
| LOLBin | Living-Off-The-Land Binary (e.g., certutil.exe, wevtutil.exe, schtasks.exe) |
| MMR | Maximal Marginal Relevance |
| OMML / SDT | Office Math Markup Language / Structured Document Tag (OpenXML) |
| RAG | Retrieval-Augmented Generation |
| RQ | Research Question (RQ1: Efficacy; RQ2: Retrieval & Failure Axes; RQ3: Depth/Cost/Latency) |
| SHA-256 | Secure Hash Algorithm 256-bit cryptographic digest |
| SOC | Security Operations Center |
| STIX | Structured Threat Information Expression (JSON schema for MITRE ATT&CK v19.2) |
| Sysmon | Microsoft Windows System Monitor (Event IDs 1, 3, 7, 12, 13, etc.) |
| TTP | Tactics, Techniques, and Procedures |

### DOCX Table Index 3 (`table_1`): Table 1: Canonical Research Questions, Hypotheses, Experimental Variables, and Evaluation Metrics.
| RQ | Research Question | Hypothesis | Independent Variable | Dependent Variable | Conditions | Primary & Diagnostic Metrics |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RQ1 | Does grounding a reasoning LLM in retrieved MITRE ATT&CK enterprise reference context improve exact technique and sub-technique attribution accuracy and 474-class Macro-F1 over an unaugmented No-RAG baseline under symmetric reasoning settings? | H1: Macro-F1(RAG) > Macro-F1(No-RAG) and Acce2e(RAG) > Acce2e(No-RAG) | Presence of retrieved ATT&CK context (No-RAG vs. RAG) | Exact ATT&CK Technique / Sub-technique attribution quality | no_rag, rag_k1, rag_k3, rag_k5, rag_k10 | Headline Accuracy (Acce2e), Valid Accuracy (Accvalid), 474-class Macro-F1, 95% Cluster-Bootstrap CI (440 clusters), Unclustered McNemar p |
| RQ2 | When attribution errors occur under RAG, how do upstream retrieval misses (Yi ∩ Ri,k = ∅) and downstream model selection discrepancies (Yi ∩ Ri,k ≠ ∅ ∧ ŷi ∉ Yi) manifest across independent measurement axes with overlaps, and how do single-event vs. contextual-event views correlate with these axes? | H2: Downstream prediction correctness is higher in the post-retrieval stratum where at least one ground-truth technique is retrieved (P(Correct \| Yi ∩ Ri,k ≠ ∅) > P(Correct \| Yi ∩ Ri,k = ∅)) | Ground-truth intersection with Top-k candidates (Yi ∩ Ri,k) & telemetry view type | Conditional prediction correctness & error locus co-occurrence | rag_k1, rag_k3, rag_k5, rag_k10 (278 single vs. 440 contextual TEST views) | Standalone Hit@k (ANY_GT_RETRIEVED), Multi-label Macro Recall@k, P(Correct \| Yi ∩ Ri,k ≠ ∅), P(Correct \| Yi ∩ Ri,k = ∅), D2i Axes |
| RQ3 | How does varying retrieval candidate depth (k ∈ {1, 3, 5, 10}) affect attribution performance, distractor susceptibility, wall-clock latency, and API token/ledger costs? | H3: Very small k (k=1) risks distractor susceptibility when retrieval misses, while larger k increases prompt token volume, latency, and cost | Retrieval candidate depth k ∈ {0, 1, 3, 5, 10} | Accuracy, Macro-F1, input/output token volume, latency, usage cost, and conservative ledger spend | 6,400 canonical dispatches across 5 conditions (1,280 × 5) | Total & Mean Input/Output Tokens, Mean & Median Latency (s), Token-Usage Cost (USD), Conservative Ledger Spend (USD) under USD 19.99 cap |

### DOCX Table Index 16 (`table_14`): Table 14: Risk Register, Severity Assessment, and Reproducibility Mitigation Controls.
| Risk Category | Severity | Mitigation Control Implemented in RAG2ATT&CK |
|---|---|---|
| Generic RAG-vs-No-RAG novelty overlap | Medium | Framed strictly as a controlled conceptual replication-and-extension study on Windows endpoint logs with exact attribution and failure decomposition; zero unsubstantiated 'first-ever' claims. |
| Label leakage from detector rules / metadata | High | Enforced INFERENCE_ALLOWLIST in src/synthetic.py retaining only sample_id and sanitized per-channel behavioral endpoint_evidence (e.g., TargetUserName, SubjectUserName, SubjectLogonId, CommandLine); purged all Sigma/Wazuh rule names, rule.mitre.*, and Contoso markers. |
| Corrupted external public telemetry (Mendeley v3) | High | Forensic Task 2 audit detected 15,713 cell discrepancies (_source.id truncation, #NAME? formulas); enforced fail-closed stop (RECONCILIATION_DIVERGENT) and bounded claims strictly to synthetic-paired-v1 under Protocol D6. |
| Class support concentration & zero denominators | Medium | Frozen 474-class Macro-F1 universe (D2d) combined with explicit NULL zero-denominator semantics (D2j) and piecewise count formula across all boundary cases; reported support-based upper bound (8/474 ≈ 1.69%). |
| API cost overrun & transient provider errors | Medium | Pre-dispatch worst-case reservation ($0.53974560/attempt), 25,600 attempt cap, deterministic exponential backoff (max_retries=3), settling at $6.5758 ($6.6284 total committed) under $19.99 ceiling. |
| Upstream API model version mutability | Low | Fixed requested model alias (gpt-5.6-luna) and inference settings across conditions; logged timestamped response IDs (run_provenance.json) and disclosed model_version: null and system_fingerprints: [] under Decision D3. |
| Prompt asymmetry across conditions | Medium | Used byte-identical prompts/baseline_v1.txt (b751fde1...) with a universal context handling clause across No-RAG and all RAG depths. |
| Post-publication irreproducibility / API cost barrier | Low | Published portable verification archive public_v4_candidate_20261003.zip (1d5f9f4b...) and scripts.reproduce_canonical_study under OFFLINE_GUARD ($0.00 API cost, < 10-12 numeric tolerance). |

## 5. Complete Verbatim Dump of All 275 Paragraphs in DOCX
**[P000 | Style: `Heading 1` [Bookmarks: _Toc244287606]]** ABSTRACTABSTRACTABSTRACT

**[P001 | Style: `Normal`]** Attributing endpoint telemetry to standardized adversary behaviors in the MITRE Adversarial Tactics, Techniques, and Common Knowledge framework is a foundational task in security operations centers and cyber threat intelligence. While modern large language models possess broad parametric knowledge, direct zero-shot prompting without external grounding risks hallucinated technique identifiers, temporal obsolescence, and unverified decisions. Retrieval-Augmented Generation grounds model reasoning in curated external knowledge, yet prior evaluations remain fragmented across unstructured threat reports, cloud or Linux logs, unpinned knowledge bases, and conflated retrieval versus output cutoffs. This study conducts a controlled replication-and-extension evaluation of knowledge-grounded retrieval augmentation for exact technique and sub-technique attribution from Windows endpoint logs under a cryptographically frozen protocol. Our design enforces strict symmetry between an unaugmented baseline and retrieval-augmented conditions across candidate depths of one, three, five, and ten retrieved documents using a fixed reasoning model alias and a dense vector index over 474 active Windows techniques from Enterprise Matrix version 19.2. Across five conditions evaluated over 1,280 synthetic paired test views (6,400 logical inference requests; 6,401 physical attempts), attribution metrics on the 718 mapped, scorable views (excluding 311 ambiguous and 251 unmapped views) show that the unaugmented baseline achieves 77.99 percent end-to-end accuracy and 0.012608 macro-averaged F1 across 474 classes, whereas retrieval augmentation at depth ten achieves 79.53 percent accuracy (an observed difference of plus 1.532 percentage points, nominal 95 percent cluster-bootstrap confidence interval from minus 2.355 to plus 5.300 percentage points, unclustered exact McNemar probability value 0.4219) and 0.014024 macro-averaged F1 (a difference of plus 0.001415, nominal 95 percent cluster-bootstrap confidence interval from plus 0.001011 to plus 0.001792), showing no statistically significant accuracy difference alongside a positive confidence interval on sparse 474-class macro-averaged F1. Independent failure decomposition shows that 80.95 percent of classification errors at depth ten (119 of 147 errors) co-occur with retrieval misses (a 55.29 percent miss rate), where accuracy is 91.28 percent when retrieved versus 70.03 percent when absent, motivating retrieval-focused improvements.Attributing endpoint telemetry to standardized adversary behaviors in the MITRE Adversarial Tactics, Techniques, and Common Knowledge framework is a foundational task in security operations centers and cyber threat intelligence. While modern large language models possess broad parametric knowledge, direct zero-shot prompting without external grounding risks hallucinated technique identifiers, temporal obsolescence, and unverified decisions. Independent failure decomposition shows that 80.95 percent of classification errors at depth ten (119 of 147 errors) co-occur with retrieval misses (a 55.29 percent miss rate), where accuracy is 91.28 percent when retrieved versus 70.03 percent when absent, motivating retrieval-focused improvements.

**[P037 | Style: `Normal`]** Enterprise defense relies heavily on the ingestion, processing, and interpretation of host endpoint telemetry. System instrumentation tools—such as Microsoft Sysmon and native Windows Security Event Auditing—record granular operating system activity, including process creation (Event ID 1 / 4688), network connections (Event ID 3), image loading (Event ID 7), registry modifications (Event IDs 12/13), service installations (Event ID 4697), user account management (Event ID 4720), and event log clearing (Event ID 1102). Security Operations Center (SOC) analysts and incident response teams continuously triage these log streams to detect malicious behavior and perform threat attribution: mapping discrete host observables to standardized adversary Tactics, Techniques, and Procedures (TTPs) defined in the MITRE ATT&CK framework [1].Enterprise defense relies heavily on the ingestion, processing, and interpretation of host endpoint telemetry. **[P038 | Style: `Normal`]** Accurate ATT&CK mapping enables automated playbooks, contextualized threat hunting, kill-chain reconstruction, and cross-organization cyber threat intelligence (CTI) sharing. However, manual attribution at the exact sub-technique level (Txxxx.yyy) is labor-intensive and cognitively demanding. An adversary executing a living-off-the-land binary (LOLBin) like certutil.exe might be staging tools from an external server (Ingress Tool Transfer: T1105) or decoding payloads locally (Deobfuscate/Decode Files or Information: T1140). Disambiguating these nuanced attacker goals requires deep domain knowledge, familiarity with command-line syntax, and comprehensive command of the evolving ATT&CK taxonomy.Accurate ATT&CK mapping enables automated playbooks, contextualized threat hunting, kill-chain reconstruction, and cross-organization cyber threat intelligence (CTI) sharing. **[P039 | Style: `Normal`]** Originally initiated from the broad capstone theme "AI Assistant for Classifying and Mapping Attack Techniques According to the MITRE ATT&CK Framework from System Logs" (Trợ lý AI phân loại và ánh xạ kỹ thuật tấn công theo khung MITRE ATT&CK từ log hệ thống), systematic literature screening revealed that while foundation reasoning models [2, 3] and Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) [4] have been widely applied to unstructured CTI report annotation [5, 6], controlled evaluations on endpoint telemetry remain scarce. Consequently, this thesis was formally refined into RAG2ATT&CK: a controlled conceptual replication-and-extension empirical study that isolates the exact effect of MITRE ATT&CK-grounded RAG [4] on Windows endpoint log technique attribution under a cryptographically frozen scientific protocol.Originally initiated from the broad capstone theme Originally initiated from the broad capstone theme "AI Assistant for Classifying and Mapping Attack Techniques According to the MITRE ATT&CK Framework from System Logs""AI Assistant for Classifying and Mapping Attack Techniques According to the MITRE ATT&CK Framework from System Logs" ( (TrTrợợ lý AI phân lo lý AI phân loạại và ánh xi và ánh xạạ k kỹỹ thu thuậật tt tấấn công theo khung MITRE ATT&CK tn công theo khung MITRE ATT&CK từừ log h log hệệ th thốốngng), systematic literature screening revealed that while foundation reasoning models [2, 3] and Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) [4] have been widely applied to unstructured CTI report annotation [5, 6], controlled evaluations on endpoint telemetry remain scarce. Consequently, this thesis was formally refined into ), systematic literature screening revealed that while foundation reasoning models [2, 3] and Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) [4] have been widely applied to unstructured CTI report annotation [5, 6], controlled evaluations on endpoint telemetry remain scarce. Consequently, this thesis was formally refined into RAG2ATT&CKRAG2ATT&CK: a controlled conceptual replication-and-extension empirical study that isolates the exact effect of MITRE ATT&CK-grounded RAG [4] on Windows endpoint log technique attribution under a cryptographically frozen scientific protocol.: a controlled conceptual replication-and-extension empirical study that isolates the exact effect of MITRE ATT&CK-grounded RAG [4] on Windows endpoint log technique attribution under a cryptographically frozen scientific protocol.

**[P040 | Style: `List Paragraph` [Bookmarks: _Toc174956223, _Toc244287613, _Toc174956224]]** Problem StatementProblem StatementProblem Statement

**[P041 | Style: `Normal`]** Commercial foundation models with extended internal reasoning capabilities (such as OpenAI's gpt-5.6-luna [2, 3]) provide native structured output enforcement and multi-step reasoning over dense technical context. Nevertheless, relying solely on an unaugmented LLM's internal parametric memory (No-RAG) or deploying naive retrieval pipelines introduces four critical scientific and operational problems:Commercial foundation models with extended internal reasoning capabilities (such as OpenAI's Commercial foundation models with extended internal reasoning capabilities (such as OpenAI's gpt-5.6-lunagpt-5.6-luna [2, 3]) provide native structured output enforcement and multi-step reasoning over dense technical context. Nevertheless, relying solely on an unaugmented LLM's internal parametric memory ( [2, 3]) provide native structured output enforcement and multi-step reasoning over dense technical context. Nevertheless, relying solely on an unaugmented LLM's internal parametric memory (No-RAGNo-RAG) or deploying naive retrieval pipelines introduces four critical scientific and operational problems:) or deploying naive retrieval pipelines introduces four critical scientific and operational problems:

**[P042 | Style: `Normal`]** 1. Parametric Hallucination, Invalid Identifiers, and Knowledge Obsolescence: Without external grounding, LLMs may synthesize non-existent technique identifiers (such as T1543.006, which is absent from the Enterprise ATT&CK taxonomy) or confuse platform scopes by assigning non-Windows techniques (such as Cloud API: T1059.009, an active cloud technique in ATT&CK v19.2 inapplicable to Windows hosts). Furthermore, static model weights cannot reflect quarterly ATT&CK updates, revocations, or deprecations, and lack auditable citations back to official procedure descriptions.1. 1. Parametric Hallucination, Invalid Identifiers, and Knowledge Obsolescence:Parametric Hallucination, Invalid Identifiers, and Knowledge Obsolescence: Without external grounding, LLMs may synthesize non-existent technique identifiers (such as  Without external grounding, LLMs may synthesize non-existent technique identifiers (such as T1543.006T1543.006, which is absent from the Enterprise ATT&CK taxonomy) or confuse platform scopes by assigning non-Windows techniques (such as Cloud API: , which is absent from the Enterprise ATT&CK taxonomy) or confuse platform scopes by assigning non-Windows techniques (such as Cloud API: T1059.009T1059.009, an active cloud technique in ATT&CK v19.2 inapplicable to Windows hosts). Furthermore, static model weights cannot reflect quarterly ATT&CK updates, revocations, or deprecations, and lack auditable citations back to official procedure descriptions., an active cloud technique in ATT&CK v19.2 inapplicable to Windows hosts). Furthermore, static model weights cannot reflect quarterly ATT&CK updates, revocations, or deprecations, and lack auditable citations back to official procedure descriptions.

**[P043 | Style: `Normal`]** 2. Lexical and Representation Gap Between Raw Endpoint Telemetry and ATT&CK Prose: Prior ATT&CK RAG systems (such as TechniqueRAG [5] and H-TechniqueRAG [6]) operate on human-written CTI reports that share rich natural-language vocabulary with MITRE ATT&CK descriptions. In contrast, Windows endpoint telemetry consists of structured, terse execution fields (CommandLine, ParentImage, TargetObject, ServiceFileName, and Windows Security Event XML attributes such as TargetUserName, SubjectUserName, and SubjectLogonId), creating a severe semantic representation gap for standard dense bi-encoder retrievers.2. 2. Lexical and Representation Gap Between Raw Endpoint Telemetry and ATT&CK Prose:Lexical and Representation Gap Between Raw Endpoint Telemetry and ATT&CK Prose: Prior ATT&CK RAG systems (such as TechniqueRAG [5] and H-TechniqueRAG [6]) operate on human-written CTI reports that share rich natural-language vocabulary with MITRE ATT&CK descriptions. In contrast, Windows endpoint telemetry consists of structured, terse execution fields ( Prior ATT&CK RAG systems (such as TechniqueRAG [5] and H-TechniqueRAG [6]) operate on human-written CTI reports that share rich natural-language vocabulary with MITRE ATT&CK descriptions. In contrast, Windows endpoint telemetry consists of structured, terse execution fields (CommandLineCommandLine, , ParentImageParentImage, , TargetObjectTargetObject, , ServiceFileNameServiceFileName, and Windows Security Event XML attributes such as , and Windows Security Event XML attributes such as TargetUserNameTargetUserName, , SubjectUserNameSubjectUserName, and , and SubjectLogonIdSubjectLogonId), creating a severe semantic representation gap for standard dense bi-encoder retrievers.), creating a severe semantic representation gap for standard dense bi-encoder retrievers.

**[P044 | Style: `Normal`]** 3. Methodological Fragmentation and Conflation of Retriever Depth (k): Prior telemetry studies either focus on binary process-tree behavior detection without exact sub-technique IDs (Yang & Hsu [7]), omit RAG entirely during command-log interpretation (CAM-LDS [8]), provide bibliographic-only Sysmon mappings without verified retrieval controls (Okuma et al. [9]), evaluate non-Windows environments without ablating retriever depth (AWS CloudTrail RAG [10], Linux eBPF Trace2ATT&CK [11]), or conflate the length of the model's emitted candidate list (Top-k output cutoff) with the depth of the external retrieval engine (k) (LADE [12]).3. 3. **[P045 | Style: `Normal`]** 4. Entangled Upstream Retrieval Misses vs. Downstream Generation Errors: Existing end-to-end evaluations rarely decouple upstream retrieval misses (Yi ∩ Ri,k = ∅) from downstream LLM selection discrepancies (Yi ∩ Ri,k ≠ ∅ ∧ ŷi ∉ Yi), leaving security engineers unable to determine how classification errors co-occur across the vector index versus the reasoning model.4. 4. **[P046 | Style: `List Paragraph` [Bookmarks: _Toc244287614, _Toc174956225]]** Research ObjectivesResearch ObjectivesResearch Objectives

**[P047 | Style: `Normal`]** The primary objective of this study is to execute a strictly controlled, reproducible empirical evaluation comparing unaugmented zero-shot reasoning (No-RAG) against MITRE ATT&CK-grounded RAG across systematic retrieval depths (k ∈ {1, 3, 5, 10}) for exact Windows technique attribution. Specifically, the research is governed by three canonical Research Questions (RQ1–RQ3) and corresponding hypotheses (H1–H3):The primary objective of this study is to execute a strictly controlled, reproducible empirical evaluation comparing unaugmented zero-shot reasoning (The primary objective of this study is to execute a strictly controlled, reproducible empirical evaluation comparing unaugmented zero-shot reasoning (No-RAGNo-RAG) against MITRE ATT&CK-grounded ) against MITRE ATT&CK-grounded RAGRAG across systematic retrieval depths ( across systematic retrieval depths (k ∈ {1, 3, 5, 10}k ∈ {1, 3, 5, 10}) for exact Windows technique ) for exact Windows technique attribution. **[P064 | Style: `Normal`]** •  Benchmark Dataset Scope (synthetic-paired-v1): The dataset comprises 670 scenario pairs (1,340 views across 1,434 unique events), split into 640 TEST pairs (1,280 views) across 52 template families and 30 DEV pairs (60 views) across 12 template families with zero family overlap. **[P066 | Style: `Normal`]** CRITICAL DISCIPLINARY CLAIM BOUNDARY & Forensic Telemetry Blocker (Task 2 / T15): All experimental evaluations and conclusions in this thesis are strictly bounded to the synthetic-paired-v1 benchmark and do NOT evaluate real-world enterprise telemetry. While the initial research plan contemplated evaluating public Windows-APT telemetry (Mendeley v3, Mozaffari et al., 2026 [13]), an internal forensic audit conducted during Task 2 (reports/final_repair_validation.md) uncovered 15,713 unresolved cell discrepancies between individual scenario CSVs and the combined dataset (14,930 cells in _source.id and 509 cells in binary event data suffering numeric precision truncation, #NAME? formula corruption in param3, and divergent timestamps). This triggered a formal fail-closed stop (RECONCILIATION_DIVERGENT). **[P080 | Style: `Normal`]** Stream 1: Retrieval-Augmented Generation for Unstructured CTI Text AnnotationStream 1: Retrieval-Augmented Generation for Unstructured CTI Text AnnotationStream 1: Retrieval-Augmented Generation for Unstructured CTI Text Annotation

**[P081 | Style: `Normal`]** TechniqueRAG (Lekssays et al., 2025) [5] introduced a retrieval-and-reranking pipeline for annotating unstructured Cyber Threat Intelligence (CTI) text reports with exact MITRE ATT&CK techniques and sub-techniques (Txxxx.yyy). The system retrieves a candidate pool of K=40 annotated text-label exemplars from TRAM and MITRE procedure corpora using BM25, reranks them via DeepSeek-v3 to select a fixed k=3 exemplars, and feeds them to a fine-tuned Ministral-8B generator. While TechniqueRAG analyzes retriever versus generator errors, it operates exclusively on natural-language CTI prose rather than host logs and does not report standalone retriever Hit@k across varying depths. H-TechniqueRAG (Morbiato et al., 2026) [6] extended CTI annotation using a hierarchical dense RAG architecture (FAISS IVF) with Llama-3-8B-Instruct over 1,200 CTI-RCM, 2,800 MITRE, and 450 TRAM samples. It routes queries from tactics to techniques, evaluating sensitivity to tactic count M (peaking at M=3 with per-tactic quota KA=15) against a zero-shot Llama 3 baseline, and highlights context distraction when excessive candidates are injected. However, H-TechniqueRAG evaluates human-authored CTI prose rather than structured endpoint telemetry and does not perform a flat retriever depth sweep k ∈ {1, 3, 5, 10}.TechniqueRAG (Lekssays et al., 2025) [5]TechniqueRAG (Lekssays et al., 2025) [5] introduced a retrieval-and-reranking pipeline for annotating unstructured Cyber Threat Intelligence (CTI) text reports with exact MITRE ATT&CK techniques and sub-techniques ( introduced a retrieval-and-reranking pipeline for annotating unstructured Cyber Threat Intelligence (CTI) text reports with exact MITRE ATT&CK techniques and sub-techniques (Txxxx.yyyTxxxx.yyy). **[P082 | Style: `Normal`]** Stream 2: Windows Sysmon and Host Command Telemetry Mapping to MITRE ATT&CKStream 2: Windows Sysmon and Host Command Telemetry Mapping to MITRE ATT&CKStream 2: Windows Sysmon and Host Command Telemetry Mapping to MITRE ATT&CK

**[P083 | Style: `Normal`]** Okuma et al. (2023) [9] established an early foundation for automated mapping of Windows Sysmon event logs to ATT&CK techniques using Atomic Red Team executions; however, as only the IEEE bibliographic record was accessible during our audit, its internal mapping mechanism remains marked UNVERIFIED. Yang & Hsu (2026) [7] represent the closest input-level comparator on Windows: their system integrates Windows Sysmon process trees, semantic matching RAG, and in-context analysis across Mistral-7B, phi-2, and TinyLlama-1.1B with a matched No-RAG baseline. Crucially, however, based on verified abstract metadata, Yang & Hsu focus on binary malicious behavior detection and natural-language explanation (reporting Precision, F1, and False Positive Rate) rather than exact 474-class ATT&CK technique/sub-technique ID attribution. LADE (Gwak et al., 2026/2027) [12] evaluates chronological host command and script traces for APT detection and ranked ATT&CK technique mapping (Top-3 and Top-10 output cutoffs) across gpt-oss-120B, Llama-3.3-70B, Mistral-Small-3.2-24B, and Gemini-2.5-Pro. Full-text verification of the 28-page manuscript (LADE-2026.pdf, pp. 13–15) confirms that LADE evaluates two benchmarks: the AVIATOR dataset (35 attack and 32 benign sequences) and a Caldera-derived dataset comprising two sets of 33 APT sequences for 2:1 and 10:1 benign-to-APT ratio settings (each set of 33 APT sequences consists of 20 adversary-profile sequences and 13 MITRE emulation-plan sequences—specifically 5 lateral-movement and 8 multi-stage attack sequences—paired with 33 benign sequences). To handle long traces (Section 4.1, pp. 7–8), LADE segments traces at 70% of each model's context window (all Caldera sequences fit in a single window; on AVIATOR, segmentation is triggered for 12 sequences on Mistral-Small-3.2-24B, 10 sequences each on gpt-oss-120B and Llama-3.3-70B under 128k windows [≈ 89,600 token limit], and 6 sequences on Gemini-2.5-Pro under a 1M window). Across 5 voting runs (p. 15), 76.3% of sequences achieve 5–0 unanimity, 13.8% achieve 4–1 consensus, and 10% split 3–2. Importantly, LADE uses rubric prompting with static ATT&CK text and performs no RAG retrieval ablation.Okuma et al. (2023) [9]Okuma et al. **[P084 | Style: `Normal`]** Stream 3: RAG-Assisted Telemetry Attribution in Cloud and Linux EnvironmentsStream 3: RAG-Assisted Telemetry Attribution in Cloud and Linux EnvironmentsStream 3: RAG-Assisted Telemetry Attribution in Cloud and Linux Environments

**[P085 | Style: `Normal`]** Adediran et al. (2026) [10] provide the closest experimental-design comparator for controlled No-RAG versus RAG contrast on security telemetry, evaluating Gemini 2.5 Pro across 200 AWS CloudTrail JSON events (122 malicious, 78 benign) using a two-step query-expansion RAG pipeline on Vertex AI. While they demonstrate that RAG improves accuracy, precision, recall, and F1 over unaugmented Gemini 2.5 Pro, their study operates on cloud API logs rather than Windows endpoint telemetry, omits numeric retrieval depth k, does not report standalone retriever Hit@k or Recall@k, and contains an unresolved internal reporting discrepancy in its published text (stating in Section 2 and the conclusion that retrieval-generation gaps account for 60% of errors, while the Section 6.4.1 heading reports 26%). Trace2ATT&CK (Lupinacci et al., 2026) [11] maps 347 Linux Atomic Red Team kernel executions (represented as eBPF provenance graphs) to ATT&CK techniques using Chroma dense retrieval (800-word ATT&CK chunks with Maximal Marginal Relevance) across six models (foundation-sec-8b, qwen3.5-9b, gpt-oss, gemma4-31b-it, deepseek-r1-qwen32b, llama3.3-70b), comparing against a prompting baseline. However, Trace2ATT&CK fixes both retriever depth and output cutoff at 5 chunks/candidates, preventing separation of standalone retriever recall from downstream generation accuracy.Adediran et al. **[P087 | Style: `Normal`]** CAM-LDS (Landauer et al., 2026) [8] evaluates zero-shot LLM log interpretation across 7 multi-step cyber attack scenarios (198 log steps across 18 Linux auditd/syslog and IDS sources) using frontier models (GPT-5.5, GPT-5.2, Llama 4, Qwen 3, Ministral per §5.1). **[P094 | Style: `Normal`]** Research Gap Synthesis: Although prior studies have investigated LLM-based ATT&CK mapping [8, 12], RAG-assisted Sysmon or security-log analysis [7], ATT&CK-grounded RAG in cloud [10] and Linux kernel telemetry [11], and retrieval/reranking for CTI text annotation [5, 6], controlled evaluations of MITRE ATT&CK-grounded RAG for exact technique and sub-technique attribution (Txxxx.yyy) from Windows endpoint telemetry remain limited. Diagnostic Extension Decoupling Retrieval vs. Generation Error Axes: Extending the retrieval-quality analysis of TechniqueRAG [5] and H-TechniqueRAG [6] to terse endpoint logs, we report both standalone sample-level hit rate (Hit@1 = 3.76%, Hit@10 = 44.71%) and fractional multi-label recall (Macro Recall@1 = 2.95%, Macro Recall@10 = 42.80%) on the 718 scorable TEST views, alongside post-retrieval stratum accuracy (91.28% when at least one ground-truth technique is retrieved vs. 70.03% when absent), showing that 80.95% (119/147) of classification errors at k=10 co-occur with retrieval misses.2. 2. **[P119 | Style: `Normal`]** Crucially, native JSON Schema enforcement (strict: true) targets the Pydantic TechniquePrediction schema ({"technique_id": "..."}) while deliberately omitting regex pattern constraints at the API schema layer. This architectural separation enables a two-layer post-hoc taxonomic validation pipeline: (1) Layer 1 (Canonical Syntax Check) validates strings against ^T\d{4}(?:\.\d{3})?$, classifying malformed strings (e.g., "T1059.1") as INVALID_ID rather than MALFORMED_RESPONSE; (2) Layer 2 (Registry Membership Check) validates syntax-compliant IDs against the pinned ATT&CK v19.2 registry. **[P128 | Style: `Normal`]** Data collection and corpus engineering in RAG2ATT&CK encompass three cryptographically audited pipelines: (1) MITRE ATT&CK v19.2 Windows knowledge base extraction, (2) forensic auditing of external public telemetry, and (3) generation and leakage-scrubbing of the synthetic-paired-v1 benchmark.Data collection and corpus engineering in RAG2ATT&CK encompass three cryptographically audited pipelines: (1) MITRE ATT&CK v19.2 Windows knowledge base extraction, (2) forensic auditing of external public telemetry, and (3) generation and leakage-scrubbing of the Data collection and corpus engineering in RAG2ATT&CK encompass three cryptographically audited pipelines: (1) MITRE ATT&CK v19.2 Windows knowledge base extraction, (2) forensic auditing of external public telemetry, and (3) generation and leakage-scrubbing of the synthetic-paired-v1synthetic-paired-v1 benchmark. benchmark.

**[P130 | Style: `Normal`]** •  Synthetic Paired Benchmark (synthetic-paired-v1): Following the Task 2 forensic rejection of Mendeley v3 CSVs (15,713 cell discrepancies), we engineered synthetic-paired-v1 (data/ground_truth/synthetic/dataset_manifest.json, SHA-256: 4576b793...), comprising 670 scenario pairs (1,340 evaluation views across 1,434 unique events). The TEST split contains 640 scenario pairs (1,280 views = 640 single-event + 640 contextual-event) across 52 template families; the DEV split contains 30 scenario pairs (60 views) across 12 template families, with strict family disjointness (FamilyTEST ∩ FamilyDEV = ∅). **[P142 | Style: `Normal`]** Statistical Inference & Independent Failure Axes (D2i): Uncertainty bounds on both accuracy differences ΔAcc = Acc(RAG) - Acc(No-RAG) and Macro-F1 differences ΔMacro-F1 = Macro-F1(RAG) - Macro-F1(No-RAG) are estimated as nominal, unadjusted 95% confidence intervals (NO_MULTIPLE_COMPARISON_CORRECTION_PRE_SPECIFIED_PRIMARY_CONTRASTS) via 1,000 cluster-bootstrap resamples (seed=42) over the 440 scenario pair clusters (cluster_unit: pair_id, preserving within-pair dependence between single and contextual views of the same scenario while treating between-pair independence across template-derived synthetic pairs as a modeling assumption). Alongside cluster-bootstrap CIs, we report exact two-sided binomial McNemar tests over discordant view pairs (b, c) at N=718 (and N=278 for complete representation pairs); we explicitly note that exact McNemar treats view-level discordant pairs as independent trials without cluster adjustment, so cluster-bootstrap CIs serve as the primary cluster-aware inference reference. **[P143 | Style: `List Paragraph` [Bookmarks: _Toc244287626]]** Limitations of the MethodologyLimitations of the MethodologyLimitations of the Methodology

**[P144 | Style: `Normal`]** Four methodological limitations and epistemic boundaries are formally enforced in this study:Four methodological limitations and epistemic boundaries are formally enforced in this study:Four methodological limitations and epistemic boundaries are formally enforced in this study:

**[P145 | Style: `Normal`]** 1. Synthetic Benchmark Regularities: While synthetic-paired-v1 guarantees verified ground truth and zero label leakage, template-derived command lines and 2–3 event contextual windows cannot capture the full background noise, obfuscation, or scale of production enterprise SOC streams.1. 1. Synthetic Benchmark Regularities:Synthetic Benchmark Regularities: While  While synthetic-paired-v1synthetic-paired-v1 guarantees verified ground truth and zero label leakage, template-derived command lines and 2–3 event contextual windows cannot capture the full background noise, obfuscation, or scale of production enterprise SOC streams. guarantees verified ground truth and zero label leakage, template-derived command lines and 2–3 event contextual windows cannot capture the full background noise, obfuscation, or scale of production enterprise SOC streams.

**[P146 | Style: `Normal`]** 2. Sparse Class Support Across the 474-Class Ontology (Support-Based vs. With-Hallucination Ceiling): Because positive ground truth in TEST is concentrated in 8 active techniques while 466 classes have zero support (F1c* = 0), unweighted 474-class Macro-F1 has a support-based upper bound of 8 / 474 ≈ 0.01688 (1.69%), and false-positive predictions landing on any of the 466 zero-support classes (Case C) further penalize the active classes' recall without ever increasing the 8-class numerator sum. We preserve the 474-class denominator without post-hoc subset rescaling to honor Protocol D2d.2. 2. **[P147 | Style: `Normal`]** 3. Epistemic Boundaries of Failure Decomposition and Post-Retrieval Stratification: Under Protocol D2i, co-occurrence of classification errors with retrieval_miss (Yi ∩ Ri,k = ∅) is an observational diagnostic association, not an interventional proof that replacing the retriever alone would eliminate all co-occurring errors. Likewise, the difference between P(Correct |  Yi ∩ Ri,k ≠ ∅) (91.28% at k=10) and P(Correct |  Yi ∩ Ri,k = ∅) (70.03%) compares two post-retrieval sample strata partitioned by retriever outcome (which correlates with intrinsic log lexical clarity) rather than a randomized intervention on identical samples.3. 3. **[P148 | Style: `Normal`]** 4. Observational Nature of Representation Contrasts: Comparisons between Single-Event (N=278) and Contextual-Event (N=440) cohorts—and across the 40 label-divergent multi-stage pairs within the 278 complete pairs—represent observational associations confounded by multi-stage scenario label expansion rather than isolated causal effects.4. 4. **[P151 | Style: `Normal`]** This chapter presents the complete empirical results obtained from the canonical live experimental campaign (experiment_id="synthetic-paired-test-1", run_id="live-66b94b1676bf46a9") executed under experiment-protocol-v1.1 at execution commit 80dbeb3fe2316e5d2d39de2ed6a5a2d15cfa9315 and bound to canonical_metric_bundle_v2.json (SHA-256: 442b5933858caafc9da3c06ee9398637213ed30d7a7db80195c0babb1195ef34) and canonical_run_seal_v1.json (SHA-256: ae7a9ada86927a79e0c6916b44d3f0ba9e1f9756df55dd7b2fce712b35d48701). Across the 5 experimental conditions (no_rag, rag_k1, rag_k3, rag_k5, rag_k10), all 1,280 TEST views were dispatched sequentially (concurrency = 1), producing 6,400 terminal execution records across 6,401 physical API attempts (6,387 VALID responses and 13 INCOMPLETE responses; 99.80% VALID fraction). **[P197 | Style: `Normal`]** •  Token-Usage Cost vs. Conservative Ledger Spend (rag_k1 vs. rag_k3 Reconciliation): Across all 1,280 logical TEST requests per condition (tariff-derived accounted costs under config/pricing_v1.json), both token-usage estimated cost and conservative ledger spend scale 4.60-fold from USD 0.4671 ($0.46714395, $0.000365/query) in no_rag to USD 2.1494 ($2.14938370, $0.001679/query) in rag_k10 (with rag_k3 at USD 1.1789 [$1.17888000] and rag_k5 at USD 1.4831 [$1.48311775]). Notably, in Table 9, rag_k1 exhibits a higher conservative ledger spend (USD 1.29723350 ≈ USD 1.30) than rag_k3 (USD 1.17888000 ≈ USD 1.18) despite consuming 42.6% fewer input tokens (1,595,554 vs. 2,780,640). Audit of budget_ledger.json and request_journal.jsonl (lines 48476–48485) explains this exact reconciliation: across the 6,400 logical requests, there were 6,401 physical API attempts, with the single extra attempt occurring on ("view_d870d574", "rag_k1") (rag_k1_predictions.jsonl line 1078). Because the failed initial attempt on view_d870d574 did not return a provider usage receipt, the financial guard (Protocol D5) conservatively retained its full worst-case pre-dispatch reservation hold of USD 0.53974560 alongside the net token-usage cost of USD 0.75748790 (pre-credit token_usage_estimated_cost_usd USD  0.75784210 less USD  0.00035420 observed cache credit), summing to USD  0.75748790 + USD  0.53974560 = USD  1.29723350. All 13 INCOMPLETE responses reaching the 8,192 output token limit occurred exclusively in non-scorable views (11 unmapped, 2 ambiguous). Total canonical conservative ledger spend was USD 6.57575890 (USD 6.62839900 total committed spend including the USD 0.05264010 pilot hold), leaving USD 13.36160100 (66.84%) in uncommitted budget beneath the USD 19.99 ceiling.•  •  Token-Usage Cost vs. Conservative Ledger Spend (Token-Usage Cost vs. Conservative Ledger Spend (rag_k1rag_k1 vs.  vs. rag_k3rag_k3 Reconciliation): Reconciliation): Across all 1,280 logical TEST requests per condition (tariff-derived accounted costs under  Across all 1,280 logical TEST requests per condition (tariff-derived accounted costs under config/pricing_v1.jsonconfig/pricing_v1.json), both token-usage estimated cost and conservative ledger spend scale ), both token-usage estimated cost and conservative ledger spend scale 4.60-fold4.60-fold from  from USD 0.4671USD 0.4671 ( ($0.46714395$0.46714395, , $0.000365$0.000365/query) in /query) in no_ragno_rag to  to USD 2.1494USD 2.1494  (($2.14938370$2.14938370, , $0.001679$0.001679/query) in /query) in rag_k10rag_k10 (with  (with rag_k3rag_k3 at  at USD 1.1789USD 1.1789 [ [$1.17888000$1.17888000] and ] and rag_k5rag_k5 at  at USD 1.4831USD 1.4831 [ [$1.48311775$1.48311775]). Candidate Pool Size and k=1 Distractor Co-occurrence (77.02% vs. 77.99% No-RAG): Because Hit@1 is only 3.76% (27/718), in 96.24% (691/718) of scorable views at rag_k1, the single injected document does not match the ground-truth technique. Presenting a single non-matching ATT&CK document coincides with an observed -0.975 pp accuracy dip relative to no_rag, whereas expanding the candidate pool to k ≥ 3 (Hit@3 = 16.43%, Hit@10 = 44.71%) coincides with higher observed accuracy (78.55% → 79.53%), consistent with the hypothesis that broader candidate sets mitigate single-document distraction.1. 1. **[P207 | Style: `List Paragraph` [Bookmarks: _Toc244287632]]** Comparison with LiteratureComparison with LiteratureComparison with Literature

**[P208 | Style: `Normal`]** Comparing RAG2ATT&CK's empirical findings against the eight audited comparators highlights why domain-specific evaluation controls are essential:Comparing RAG2ATT&CK's empirical findings against the eight audited comparators highlights why domain-specific evaluation controls are essential:Comparing RAG2ATT&CK's empirical findings against the eight audited comparators highlights why domain-specific evaluation controls are essential:

**[P209 | Style: `Normal`]** •  Contrast with CloudTrail RAG (Adediran et al., 2026) [10]: Whereas Adediran et al. reported large end-to-end RAG gains on 200 AWS CloudTrail events using Gemini 2.5 Pro and a two-step query-expansion pipeline on Vertex AI, our single-step dense retrieval on raw Windows logs with gpt-5.6-luna yields a modest, statistically non-significant +1.532 pp accuracy difference alongside a +0.001415 Macro-F1 difference. Because the two studies differ across telemetry domain, model family, and retrieval pipeline architecture, this contrast suggests the hypothesis—rather than proving causally in isolation—that single-step dense retrieval over raw Windows OS fields faces a steeper lexical-semantic representation gap than query-expanded cloud API logs.•  •  Contrast with CloudTrail RAG (Adediran et al., 2026) [10]:Contrast with CloudTrail RAG (Adediran et al., 2026) [10]: Whereas Adediran et al. reported large end-to-end RAG gains on 200 AWS CloudTrail events using Gemini 2.5 Pro and a two-step query-expansion pipeline on Vertex AI, our single-step dense retrieval on raw Windows logs with  Whereas Adediran et al. reported large end-to-end RAG gains on 200 AWS CloudTrail events using Gemini 2.5 Pro and a two-step query-expansion pipeline on Vertex AI, our single-step dense retrieval on raw Windows logs with gpt-5.6-lunagpt-5.6-luna yields a modest, statistically non-significant  yields a modest, statistically non-significant +1.532 pp+1.532 pp accuracy difference alongside a  accuracy difference alongside a +0.001415+0.001415 Macro-F1 difference. Because the two studies differ across telemetry domain, model family, and retrieval pipeline architecture, this contrast suggests the hypothesis—rather than proving causally in isolation—that single-step dense retrieval over raw Windows OS fields faces a steeper lexical-semantic representation gap than query-expanded cloud API logs. **[P211 | Style: `Normal`]** •  Advancement over Fixed-Cutoff Telemetry Studies (Trace2ATT&CK [11], CAM-LDS [8], LADE [12]): Unlike Trace2ATT&CK [11] (which fixes both retriever depth and output cutoff at 5 on Linux eBPF graphs) and CAM-LDS [8] / LADE [12] (which vary output cutoff Top-k without RAG), RAG2ATT&CK's decoupled ablation across k ∈ {1, 3, 5, 10} separately characterizes input token growth (7.58x at k=10), ledger cost scaling (4.60x at k=10), sample-level Hit@k (44.71% at k=10), and multi-label Macro Recall@k (42.80% at k=10).•  •  Advancement over Fixed-Cutoff Telemetry Studies (Trace2ATT&CK [11], CAM-LDS [8], LADE [12]):Advancement over Fixed-Cutoff Telemetry Studies (Trace2ATT&CK [11], CAM-LDS [8], LADE [12]): Unlike Trace2ATT&CK [11] (which fixes both retriever depth and output cutoff at 5 on Linux eBPF graphs) and CAM-LDS [8] / LADE [12] (which vary output cutoff  Unlike Trace2ATT&CK [11] (which fixes both retriever depth and output cutoff at 5 on Linux eBPF graphs) and CAM-LDS [8] / LADE [12] (which vary output cutoff Top-kTop-k without RAG), RAG2ATT&CK's decoupled ablation across  without RAG), RAG2ATT&CK's decoupled ablation across k ∈ {1, 3, 5, 10}k ∈ {1, 3, 5, 10} separately characterizes input token growth (7.58x at  separately characterizes input token growth (7.58x at k=10k=10), ledger cost scaling (4.60x at ), ledger cost scaling (4.60x at k=10k=10), sample-level ), sample-level Hit@kHit@k (44.71% at  (44.71% at k=10k=10), and multi-label ), and multi-label Macro Recall@kMacro Recall@k (42.80% at  (42.80% at k=10k=10).).

**[P212 | Style: `List Paragraph` [Bookmarks: _Toc244287633]]** Implications of the ResultsImplications of the ResultsImplications of the Results

**[P213 | Style: `Normal`]** For SOC architects and cybersecurity AI practitioners, our diagnostic findings motivate three testable engineering hypotheses for production RAG design:For SOC architects and cybersecurity AI practitioners, our diagnostic findings motivate three testable engineering hypotheses for production RAG design:For SOC architects and cybersecurity AI practitioners, our diagnostic findings motivate three testable engineering hypotheses for production RAG design:

**[P214 | Style: `Normal`]** 1. Prioritize Retrieval Engineering Before Scaling Context Windows: Because 80.95% (119/147) of errors at k=10 co-occur with retrieval misses—while accuracy in the retrieved stratum reaches 91.28%—expanding context depth from k=0 to k=10 increases input token volume by 7.58x and monetary cost by 4.60x while leaving 55.29% of queries without ground-truth context. Improving upstream candidate recall is therefore the highest-leverage intervention to test next.1. 1. **[P227 | Style: `Normal`]** Synthetic Data Boundaries & Public Telemetry Governance: While synthetic-paired-v1 isolates retrieval mechanics cleanly, synthetic templates lack the extreme background noise, custom administrative scripts, and adversarial obfuscation of live enterprise networks. Furthermore, our Task 2 forensic discovery of 15,713 cell discrepancies in the Mendeley v3 Windows-APT dataset [13] underscores a broader lesson for cybersecurity machine learning: public telemetry benchmarks must never be ingested without cell-level reconciliation and cryptographic provenance verification.Synthetic Data Boundaries & Public Telemetry Governance:Synthetic Data Boundaries & Public Telemetry Governance: While  While synthetic-paired-v1synthetic-paired-v1 isolates retrieval mechanics cleanly, synthetic templates lack the extreme background noise, custom administrative scripts, and adversarial obfuscation of live enterprise networks. Furthermore, our Task 2 forensic discovery of 15,713 cell discrepancies in the Mendeley v3 Windows-APT dataset [13] underscores a broader lesson for cybersecurity machine learning: public telemetry benchmarks must never be ingested without cell-level reconciliation and cryptographic provenance verification.

Bitwise determinism holds 100% for dataset regeneration, FAISS indexing, and discrete evaluation counts; across repeated 1,000-resample bootstrap runs (seed=42), all integer counts are invariant, arithmetic invariants hold within < 10-12, and the 4 Macro-CI endpoints exhibit floating-point variation < 6 × 10-18. **[P240 | Style: `List Paragraph` [Bookmarks: _Toc244287639]]** Future WorkFuture Future WorkWork

**[P241 | Style: `Normal`]** Building directly upon the observational diagnostics and bottlenecks identified in this thesis, we formulate four concrete hypotheses for future experimental validation:Building directly upon the observational diagnostics and bottlenecks identified in this thesis, we formulate four concrete hypotheses for future experimental validation:Building directly upon the observational diagnostics and bottlenecks identified in this thesis, we formulate four concrete hypotheses for future experimental validation:

**[P242 | Style: `Normal`]** 1. Hybrid Lexical-Dense Retrieval and Schema-Aware Query Expansion: Evaluate whether combining sparse lexical matching (BM25 over Windows Event IDs, registry paths, and LOLBin command flags) with dense embeddings via Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF) and cross-encoder reranking—augmented by a schema translation layer mapping Windows Security Event ID 4720 attributes (TimeCreated, Computer, EventID, TargetUserName, SubjectUserName, SubjectLogonId) and local-account CLI patterns to canonical ATT&CK concepts (T1136.001)—improves recall on zero-hit techniques.1. 1. Hybrid Lexical-Dense Retrieval and Schema-Aware Query Expansion:Hybrid Lexical-Dense Retrieval and Schema-Aware Query Expansion: Evaluate whether combining sparse lexical matching (BM25 over Windows Event IDs, registry paths, and LOLBin command flags) with dense embeddings via Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF) and cross-encoder reranking—augmented by a schema translation layer mapping Windows Security Event ID  Evaluate whether combining sparse lexical matching (BM25 over Windows Event IDs, registry paths, and LOLBin command flags) with dense embeddings via Reciprocal Rank Fusion (RRF) and cross-encoder reranking—augmented by a schema translation layer mapping Windows Security Event ID 47204720 attributes ( attributes (TimeCreatedTimeCreated, , ComputerComputer, , EventIDEventID, , TargetUserNameTargetUserName, , SubjectUserNameSubjectUserName, , SubjectLogonIdSubjectLogonId) and local-account CLI patterns to canonical ATT&CK concepts () and local-account CLI patterns to canonical ATT&CK concepts (T1136.001T1136.001)—improves recall on zero-hit techniques.)—improves recall on zero-hit techniques.

**[P243 | Style: `Normal`]** 2. Automated Event Pre-Filtering to Test the Context-Dilution Hypothesis: Test experimentally whether an upstream event salience filter or per-event max-pooling aggregator before vector encoding improves multi-event retrieval ranks compared to single-vector window pooling.2. 2. Automated Event Pre-Filtering to Test the Context-Dilution Hypothesis:Automated Event Pre-Filtering to Test the Context-Dilution Hypothesis: Test experimentally whether an upstream event salience filter or per-event max-pooling aggregator before vector encoding improves multi-event retrieval ranks compared to single-vector window pooling. Test experimentally whether an upstream event salience filter or per-event max-pooling aggregator before vector encoding improves multi-event retrieval ranks compared to single-vector window pooling.

**[P244 | Style: `Normal`]** 3. Verified Real-World Windows Enterprise Telemetry Benchmarking (Task T15 Follow-Up): Construct and cryptographically seal a real-world Windows intrusion telemetry benchmark collected directly from live Caldera / Atomic Red Team range executions with raw EVTX/JSON provenance hashes, overcoming the 15,713 CSV export discrepancies discovered in Mendeley v3 [13].3. 3. **[P245 | Style: `Normal`]** 4. Expanded Taxonomic Coverage and Multi-Model Cost-Tiered Routing: Expand positive benchmark support from 8 techniques to 50+ Windows techniques across the 474-technique matrix, and evaluate a tiered SOC routing architecture pairing fast open-weight models (Llama-3.3-70B, Qwen-3) on high-confidence hits with frontier reasoning models (gpt-5.6-luna) reserved for ambiguous escalations.4. 4.
